[1] https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smith_normal_form#Algorithm\n    .. [2] http://sierra.nmsu.edu/morandi/notes/SmithNormalForm.pdf\n\n    '''\n    dM = _to_domain(m, domain)\n    factors = _invf(dM)\n    factors = tuple(dM.domain.to_sympy(f) for f in factors)\n    # XXX: deprecated.\n    if hasattr(m, \"ring\"):\n        if m.ring.is_PolynomialRing:\n            K = m.ring\n            to_poly = lambda f: Poly(f, K.symbols, domain=K.domain)\n            factors = tuple(to_poly(f) for f in factors)\n    return factors\n\n\ndef hermite_normal_form(A, *, D=None, check_rank=False):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of a Matrix *A* of integers.\n\n    Examples\n    ========\n\n    >>> from sympy import Matrix\n    >>> from sympy.matrices.normalforms import hermite_normal_form\n    >>> m = Matrix([[12, 6, 4], [3, 9, 6], [2, 16, 14]])\n    >>> print(hermite_normal_form(m))\n    Matrix([[10, 0, 2], [0, 15, 3], [0, 0, 2]])\n\n    Parameters\n    ==========\n\n    A : $m \\times n$ ``Matrix`` of integers.\n\n    D : int, optional\n        Let $W$ be the HNF of *A*. If known in advance, a positive integer *D*\n        being any multiple of $\\det(W)$ may be provided. In this case, if *A*\n        also has rank $m$, then we may use an alternative algorithm that works\n        mod *D* in order to prevent coefficient explosion.\n\n    check_rank : boolean, optional (default=False)\n        The basic assumption is that, if you pass a value for *D*, then\n        you already believe that *A* has rank $m$, so we do not waste time\n        checking it for you. If you do want this to be checked (and the\n        ordinary, non-modulo *D* algorithm to be used if the check fails), then\n        set *check_rank* to ``True``.\n\n    Returns\n    =======\n\n    ``Matrix``\n        The HNF of matrix *A*.\n\n    Raises\n    ======\n\n    DMDomainError\n        If the domain of the matrix is not :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    DMShapeError\n        If the mod *D* algorithm is used but the matrix has more rows than\n        columns.\n\n    References\n    ==========\n\n    .. [1] Cohen, H. *A Course in Computational Algebraic Number Theory.*\n       (See Algorithms 2.4.5 and 2.4.8.)\n\n    \"\"\"\n    # Accept any of Python int, SymPy Integer, and ZZ itself:\n    if D is not None and not ZZ.of_type(D):\n        D = ZZ(int(D))\n    return _hnf(A._rep, D=D, check_rank=check_rank).to_Matrix()\n"}]},"isError":false}

In the algorithms for computing HNF,\n    it is critical that x, y not only satisfy the condition of being small\n    in magnitude -- namely that |x| <= |b|/g, |y| <- |a|/g -- but also that\n    y == 0 when a | b.\n\n    \"\"\"\n    x, y, g = ZZ.gcdex(a, b)\n    if a != 0 and b % a == 0:\n        y = 0\n        x = -1 if a < 0 else 1\n    return x, y, g\n\n\ndef _hermite_normal_form(A):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of DomainMatrix *A* over :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Parameters\n    ==========\n\n    A : :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix` over domain :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Returns\n    =======\n\n    :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix`\n        The HNF of matrix *A*.\n\n    Raises\n    ======\n\n    DMDomainError\n        If the domain of the matrix is not :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    References\n    ==========\n\n    .. The total number of rows we will consider is min(m, n), where\n    # A is an m x n matrix.\n    m, n = A.shape\n    rows = min(m, n)\n    A = A.to_dense().rep.copy()\n    # Our goal is to put pivot entries in the rightmost columns.\n    # Invariant: Before processing each row, k should be the index of the\n    # leftmost column in which we have so far put a pivot.\n    k = n\n    for i in range(m - 1, m - 1 - rows, -1):\n        k -= 1\n        # k now points to the column in which we want to put a pivot.\n        # We want zeros in all entries to the left of the pivot column.\n        for j in range(k - 1, -1, -1):\n            if A[i][j] != 0:\n                # Replace cols j, k by lin combs of these cols such that, in row i,\n                # col j has 0, while col k has the gcd of their row i entries. Note\n                # that this ensures a nonzero entry in col k.\n                u, v, d = _gcdex(A[i][k], A[i][j])\n                r, s = A[i][k] // d, A[i][j] // d\n                add_columns(A, k, j, u, v, -s, r)\n        b = A[i][k]\n        # Do not want the pivot entry to be negative.\n        if b < 0:\n            add_columns(A, k, k, -1, 0, -1, 0)\n            b = -b\n        # The pivot entry will be 0 iff the row was 0 from the pivot col all the\n        # way to the left. In this case, we are still working on the same pivot\n        # col for the next row. 1, -1, -1):\n        k -= 1\n        for j in range(k - 1, -1, -1):\n            if A[i][j] != 0:\n                u, v, d = _gcdex(A[i][k], A[i][j])\n                r, s = A[i][k] // d, A[i][j] // d\n                add_columns_mod_R(A, R, k, j, u, v, -s, r)\n        b = A[i][k]\n        if b == 0:\n            A[i][k] = b = R\n        u, v, d = _gcdex(b, R)\n        for ii in range(m):\n            W[ii][i] = u*A[ii][k] % R\n        if W[i][i] == 0:\n            W[i][i] = R\n        for j in range(i + 1, m):\n            q = W[i][j] // W[i][i]\n            add_columns(W, j, i, 1, -q, 0, 1)\n        R //= d\n    return DomainMatrix(W, (m, m), ZZ).to_dense()\n\n\ndef hermite_normal_form(A, *, D=None, check_rank=False):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix` *A* over\n    :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Examples\n    ========\n\n    >>> from sympy import ZZ\n    >>> from sympy.polys.matrices import DomainMatrix\n    >>> from sympy.polys.matrices.normalforms import hermite_normal_form\n    >>> m = DomainMatrix([[ZZ(12), ZZ(6), ZZ(4)],\n    ... If m < n, there are at most m pivots in the bottom m rows.\n\nBut the issue here: the algorithm iterates `min(m, n)` times starting from the bottom, which means the TOP m - min(m,n) rows are never touched. For a matrix with m ≥ n, that's m - n rows at top that are never touched. These rows are then assumed to be zero rows (or at least, whatever's in them in the leftmost columns gets dropped since we return A[:, k:]).\n\nIn our example, m=3, n=2, so the top row (row 0) is never touched. The algorithm returns A[:, 1:] (dropping column 0). Row 0 = [1, 12] → after dropping col 0, it becomes [12]. In this case, we are still working on the same pivot\n        # col for the next row. Note\n                # that this ensures a nonzero entry in col k.\n                u, v, d = _gcdex(A[i][k], A[i][j])\n                r, s = A[i][k] // d, A[i][j] // d\n                add_columns(A, k, j, u, v, -s, r)\n        b = A[i][k]\n        # Do not want the pivot entry to be negative.\n        if b < 0:\n            add_columns(A, k, k, -1, 0, -1, 0)\n            b = -b\n        # The pivot entry will be 0 iff the row was 0 from the pivot col all the\n        # way to the left. In this case, we are still working on the same pivot\n        # col for the next row. Therefore:\n        if b == 0:\n            k += 1\n        # If the pivot entry is nonzero, then we want to reduce all entries to its\n        # right in the sense of the division algorithm, i.e. make them all remainders\n        # w.r.t. the pivot as divisor.\n        else:\n            for j in range(k + 1, n):\n                q = A[i][j] // b\n                add_columns(A, j, k, 1, -q, 0, 1)\n    # Finally, the HNF consists of those columns of A in which we succeeded in making\n    # a nonzero pivot.\n    return DomainMatrix.from_rep(A)[:, k:]\n```\n\nHmm, in current sympy master, I believe the loop is `for i in range(m - 1, -1, -1)` — i.e., ALL rows, not just min(m, n) rows. Hmm, e.g., A = [[1, 0, 5], [0, 1, 3]]: r = 2, n - r = 1, result = [[1, 0], [0, 1]] which is 2×2 = m×m. OK. Clear j in [1, 0]: A[1][1] = 0, A[1][0] = 0, skip. b = 0 → k += 1 → k = 2.\n- i=0: k=1. Row 0 = [1, 0, 5]. j in [0]: A[0][0] = 1 ≠ 0: _gcdex(A[0][1], A[0][0]) = _gcdex(0, 1) = (0, 1, 1), r, s = 0, 1. add_columns(A, 1, 0, 0, 1, -1, 0): col1 := col0; col0 := -col1 + 0*col0.\n  - Row 0: [1, 0, 5]: col1 = 1, col0 = 0 → [0, 1, 5].\n  - Row 1: [0, 0, 0] → [0, 0, 0].\n  - b = A[0][1] = 1. Return A[:, 1:] = [[1, 0], [0, 0]] — 2×2. HNF = [[1, 0], [0, 0]]. Reasonable.\n\nOK so with the \"process all rows\" fix, the algorithm returns the correct HNF. [1] Cohen, H. *A Course in Computational Algebraic Number Theory.*\n       (See Algorithm 2.4.5.)\n\n    \"\"\"\n    if not A.domain.is_ZZ:\n        raise DMDomainError('Matrix must be over domain ZZ.')\n    # We work one row at a time, starting from the bottom row, and working our\n    # way up.\n    m, n = A.shape\n    A = A.to_dense().rep.to_ddm().copy()\n    # Our goal is to put pivot entries in the rightmost columns.\n    # Invariant: Before processing each row, k should be the index of the\n    # leftmost column in which we have so far put a pivot.\n    k = n\n    for i in range(m - 1, -1, -1):\n        if k == 0:\n            # This case can arise when n < m and we've already found n pivots.\n            # We don't need to consider any more rows, because this is already\n            # the maximum possible number of pivots.\n            break\n        k -= 1\n        # k now points to the column in which we want to put a pivot.\n        # We want zeros in all entries to the left of the pivot column.\n        for j in range(k - 1, -1, -1):\n            if A[i][j] != 0:\n                # Replace cols j, k by lin combs of these cols such that, in row i,\n                # col j has 0, while col k has the gcd of their row i entries. Note\n                # that this ensures a nonzero entry in col k.\n                u, v, d = _gcdex(A[i][k], A[i][j])\n                r, s = A[i][k] // d, A[i][j] // d\n                add_columns(A, k, j, u, v, -s, r)\n        b = A[i][k]\n        # Do not want the pivot entry to be negative.\n        if b < 0:\n            add_columns(A, k, k, -1, 0, -1, 0)\n            b = -b\n        # The pivot entry will be 0 iff the row was 0 from the pivot col all the\n        # way to the left. In this case, we are still working on the same pivot\n        # col for the next row. Therefore:\n        if b == 0:\n            k += 1\n        # If the pivot entry is nonzero, then we want to reduce all entries to its\n        # right in the sense of the division algorithm, i.e. make them all remainders\n        # w.r.t. the pivot as divisor.\n        else:\n            for j in range(k + 1, n):\n                q = A[i][j] // b\n                add_columns(A, j, k, 1, -q, 0, 1)\n    # Finally, the HNF consists of those columns of A in which we succeeded in making\n    # a nonzero pivot.\n    return DomainMatrix.from_rep(A.to_dfm_or_ddm())[:, k:]\n\n\ndef _hermite_normal_form_modulo_D(A, D):\n"}],"details":{}}}

Therefore:\n        if b == 0:\n            k += 1\n        # If the pivot entry is nonzero, then we want to reduce all entries to its\n        # right in the sense of the division algorithm, i.e. make them all remainders\n        # w.r.t. the pivot as divisor.\n        else:\n            for j in range(k + 1, n):\n                q = A[i][j] // b\n                add_columns(A, j, k, 1, -q, 0, 1)\n    # Finally, the HNF consists of those columns of A in which we succeeded in making\n    # a nonzero pivot.\n    return DomainMatrix.from_rep(A.to_dfm_or_ddm())[:, k:]\n\n\ndef _hermite_normal_form_modulo_D(A, D):\n"}],"isError":false,"timestamp":1786866212363}]}

{"type":"message_end","message":{"role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"The fix is complete and minimal. Let me summarize:\n\n**Root cause:** `_hermite_normal_form` in `sympy/polys/matrices/normalforms.py` only processed the bottom `min(m, n)` rows. When a matrix has more rows than columns (`m > n`) and the pivots can't all be found in the bottom `n` rows (e.g., `[[1, 12], [0, 8], [0, 5]]` where rows 1 and 2 are zero in column 0), the algorithm silently dropped the unprocessed top rows, effectively treating the matrix as rank-deficient and removing rows/columns.\n\n**Fix:** Process all rows from bottom to top, breaking early once a pivot has been placed in every column (`k == 0`). This matches the fix that went into SymPy 1.13.\n\n**Tests updated:** The old test `[[2, 7], [0, 0], [0, 0]]` → `3×0` locked in the buggy behavior (rank-1 matrix with an empty HNF); it now correctly returns `[[1], [0], [0]]`. Only the bottom 2 rows were considered — but both have `0` in column 0, so no pivot could be placed there, and row 0 (which contains the pivot for column 0) was never processed. The algorithm concluded rank 1, dropped column 0, and returned `[[12], [8], [5]]`, which is why after the transposes/flips you got a single row.\n\n## Fix\n\nProcess **all** rows from bottom to top, breaking early once a pivot has been placed in every column (`k == 0`), which can only happen when `n < m` and all `n` pivots are already found:\n\n```python\nfor i in range(m - 1, -1, -1):\n    if k == 0:\n        # This case can arise when n < m and we've already found n pivots.\n        break\n    k -= 1\n```\n\n## Tests\n\n- Updated the `[[2, 7], [0, 0], [0, 0]]` test in both `sympy/polys/matrices/tests/test_normalforms.py` and `sympy/matrices/tests/test_normalforms.py` — it previously asserted a `3×0` result (which was itself a symptom of the bug: a rank-1 matrix's HNF shouldn't be empty); it now correctly expects `[[1], [0], [0]]`.\n- Added a regression test for your exact reported case.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Your expression now returns `[[5, 8, 0], [0, 0, 1]]` as expected.\n- All 5 normalform tests pass, plus 52 numberfields `modules.py` tests (the other consumer of HNF).\n- 300 randomized matrices: HNF idempotence (lattice invariance) holds with no failures."}],"api":"openai-completions","provider":"opencode-go","model":"deepseek-v4-flash","usage":{"input":1008,"output":815,"cacheRead":45952,"cacheWrite":0,"reasoning":311,"totalTokens":47775,"cost":{"input":0.00007056,"output":0.00011410000000000001,"cacheRead":0.0000643328,"cacheWrite":0,"total":0.0002489928}},"stopReason":"stop","timestamp":1786866309629,"responseId":"60508a16-2fd5-41f6-817b-f426edfebf8c","rawStopReason":"stop"}}

In the algorithms for computing HNF,\n    it is critical that x, y not only satisfy the condition of being small\n    in magnitude -- namely that |x| <= |b|/g, |y| <- |a|/g -- but also that\n    y == 0 when a | b.\n\n    \"\"\"\n    x, y, g = ZZ.gcdex(a, b)\n    if a != 0 and b % a == 0:\n        y = 0\n        x = -1 if a < 0 else 1\n    return x, y, g\n\n\ndef _hermite_normal_form(A):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of DomainMatrix *A* over :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Parameters\n    ==========\n\n    A : :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix` over domain :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    Returns\n    =======\n\n    :py:class:`~.DomainMatrix`\n        The HNF of matrix *A*.\n\n    Raises\n    ======\n\n    DMDomainError\n        If the domain of the matrix is not :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    References\n    ==========\n\n    .. Let me look at the current sympy master code:\n\n```python\ndef _hermite_normal_form(A):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of DomainMatrix *A* over :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    ...\n    \"\"\"\n    if not A.domain.is_ZZ:\n        raise DMDomainError('Matrix must be over domain ZZ.')\n    # We work one row at a time, starting from the bottom row, and working our\n    # way up.\n    m, n = A.shape\n    A = A.to_dense().rep.copy()\n    # Our goal is to put pivot entries in the rightmost columns.\n    # Invariant: Before processing each row, k should be the index of the\n    # leftmost column in which we have so far put a pivot.\n    k = n\n    for i in range(m - 1, -1, -1):\n        k -= 1\n        # k now points to the column in which we want to put a pivot.\n        # We want zeros in all entries to the left of the pivot column.\n        for j in range(k - 1, -1, -1):\n            if A[i][j] != 0:\n                # Replace cols j, k by lin combs of these cols such that, in row i,\n                # col j has 0, while col k has the gcd of their row i entries. Note\n                # that this ensures a nonzero entry in col k.\n                u, v, d = _gcdex(A[i][k], A[i][j])\n                r, s = A[i][k] // d, A[i][j] // d\n                add_columns(A, k, j, u, v, -s, r)\n        b = A[i][k]\n        # Do not want the pivot entry to be negative.\n        if b < 0:\n            add_columns(A, k, k, -1, 0, -1, 0)\n            b = -b\n        # The pivot entry will be 0 iff the row was 0 from the pivot col all the\n        # way to the left. In this case, we are still working on the same pivot\n        # col for the next row. Therefore:\n        if b == 0:\n            k += 1\n        # If the pivot entry is nonzero, then we want to reduce all entries to its\n        # right in the sense of the division algorithm, i.e. make them all remainders\n        # w.r.t. the pivot as divisor.\n        else:\n            for j in range(k + 1, n):\n                q = A[i][j] // b\n                add_columns(A, j, k, 1, -q, 0, 1)\n    # Finally, the HNF consists of those columns of A in which we succeeded in making\n    # a nonzero pivot.\n    return DomainMatrix.from_rep(A)[:, k:]\n```\n\nHmm, in current sympy master, I believe the loop is `for i in range(m - 1, -1, -1)` — i.e., ALL rows, not just min(m, n) rows. Let me check what the actual sympy fix was for this issue.\n\nThe actual SymPy issue is #25461: \"hermite_normal_form removes rows\". Let me recall the fix. I believe the fix was PR #25467 or similar. Let me think about the actual fix in sympy master.\n\nLooking at current sympy master `sympy/polys/matrices/normalforms.py`:\n\n```python\ndef _hermite_normal_form(A):\n    r\"\"\"\n    Compute the Hermite Normal Form of DomainMatrix *A* over :ref:`ZZ`.\n\n    ...\n    \"\"\"\n    if not A.domain.is_ZZ:\n        raise DMDomainError('Matrix must be over domain ZZ.')\n    # We work one row at a time, starting from the bottom row, and working our\n    # way up.\n    m, n = A.shape\n    A = A.to_dense().rep.copy()\n    # Our goal is to put pivot entries in the rightmost columns.\n    # Invariant: Before processing each row, k should be the index of the\n    # leftmost column in which we have so far put a pivot.\n    k = n\n    for i in range(m - 1, -1, -1):\n        k -= 1\n        # k now points to the column in which we want to put a pivot.\n        # We want zeros in all entries to the left of the pivot column.\n        for j in range(k - 1, -1, -1):\n            if A[i][j] != 0:\n                # Replace cols j, k by lin combs of these cols such that, in row i,\n                # col j has 0, while col k has the gcd of their row i entries. Note\n                # that this ensures a nonzero entry in col k.\n                u, v, d = _gcdex(A[i][k], A[i][j])\n                r, s = A[i][k] // d, A[i][j] // d\n                add_columns(A, k, j, u, v, -s, r)\n        b = A[i][k]\n        # Do not want the pivot entry to be negative.\n        if b < 0:\n            add_columns(A, k, k, -1, 0, -1, 0)\n            b = -b\n        # The pivot entry will be 0 iff the row was 0 from the pivot col all the\n        # way to the left. In this case, we are still working on the same pivot\n        # col for the next row. Let me summarize:\n\n**Root cause:** `_hermite_normal_form` in `sympy/polys/matrices/normalforms.py` only processed the bottom `min(m, n)` rows. When a matrix has more rows than columns (`m > n`) and the pivots can't all be found in the bottom `n` rows (e.g., `[[1, 12], [0, 8], [0, 5]]` where rows 1 and 2 are zero in column 0), the algorithm silently dropped the unprocessed top rows, effectively treating the matrix as rank-deficient and removing rows/columns.\n\n**Fix:** Process all rows from bottom to top, breaking early once a pivot has been placed in every column (`k == 0`). This matches the fix that went into SymPy 1.13.\n\n**Tests updated:** The old test `[[2, 7], [0, 0], [0, 0]]` → `3×0` locked in the buggy behavior (rank-1 matrix with an empty HNF); it now correctly returns `[[1], [0], [0]]`. Only the bottom 2 rows were considered — but both have `0` in column 0, so no pivot could be placed there, and row 0 (which contains the pivot for column 0) was never processed. The algorithm concluded rank 1, dropped column 0, and returned `[[12], [8], [5]]`, which is why after the transposes/flips you got a single row.\n\n## Fix\n\nProcess **all** rows from bottom to top, breaking early once a pivot has been placed in every column (`k == 0`), which can only happen when `n < m` and all `n` pivots are already found:\n\n```python\nfor i in range(m - 1, -1, -1):\n    if k == 0:\n        # This case can arise when n < m and we've already found n pivots.\n        break\n    k -= 1\n```\n\n## Tests\n\n- Updated the `[[2, 7], [0, 0], [0, 0]]` test in both `sympy/polys/matrices/tests/test_normalforms.py` and `sympy/matrices/tests/test_normalforms.py` — it previously asserted a `3×0` result (which was itself a symptom of the bug: a rank-1 matrix's HNF shouldn't be empty); it now correctly expects `[[1], [0], [0]]`.\n- Added a regression test for your exact reported case.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Your expression now returns `[[5, 8, 0], [0, 0, 1]]` as expected.\n- All 5 normalform tests pass, plus 52 numberfields `modules.py` tests (the other consumer of HNF).\n- 300 randomized matrices: HNF idempotence (lattice invariance) holds with no failures."}],"api":"openai-completions","provider":"opencode-go","model":"deepseek-v4-flash","usage":{"input":1008,"output":815,"cacheRead":45952,"cacheWrite":0,"reasoning":311,"totalTokens":47775,"cost":{"input":0.00007056,"output":0.00011410000000000001,"cacheRead":0.0000643328,"cacheWrite":0,"total":0.0002489928}},"stopReason":"stop","timestamp":1786866309629,"responseId":"60508a16-2fd5-41f6-817b-f426edfebf8c","rawStopReason":"stop"}],"willRetry":false}
